# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-17

30 complete documentation guides and 52 additional screened creative-AI tools/components. 11 earlier screened entries now have guides; these are review completions, not new-launch claims. Workflows include editable agent-authored pixel assets, game art, transcript-based editing, local music sketches, typography/story prompting, OCR and VFX handoff. Every admission has a complete current software-grant review and six explicit quality checks. Required proprietary hosts, restricted/unclear weights and paid services are separated from the code license. Nothing discovered was installed or run. Final query metadata adds textile reconstruction, local filmmaking, agent capture/synchronization and depth-based temporal VFX. A WIF matrix round-trip is not a physical cloth trial, relative depth is not metric geometry, and MiniMax-H3 territory/output terms and Qwen-Image2.1 noncommercial terms are separate from the application’s open code grant. An audio-to-MIDI/ML roadmap without the implemented AI feature stays local. Nine existing-library source follow-ups recover five complete software grants from GitLab without promoting their quality tiers; one current low-poly portrait repository contains only README and LICENSE, so its setup check is explicitly failed. Codeberg bot challenges and unavailable OpenSlides sources keep affected checks unknown. The prior 3000-entry library already has this week’s cited whole-library source reviews; these follow-ups and new records extend that coverage, rather than claiming3000 new reviews today. Final weekly completeness is checked after the library refresh. The full frozen discovery plan covers 108 fields,1177 repository queries and 27 model tasks. It carries every prior term/lane, adds an agent-pixel-art field, event-camera/caustic terms and image classification/feature extraction, deepens74 formerly bounded queries and adds120 date partitions for 40 capped terms. All three repository lanes remain active. Live primary research beyond GitHub supplements every field and all eight configured ecosystems. Metadata-level screening includes the complete retained catalog and historical pilot entries, followed by targeted document/code/full-license reviews beyond the automatic README shortlist; raw unreviewed/excluded records stay local. Two earlier unsealed Codeberg bot-challenge bodies were superseded during recapture; their original attempt metadata is retained with that archival limitation. Later challenge captures and failed source requests are preserved, and no quality or license claim relies on the missing bodies. First-hand support reports add concrete Windows plugin-discovery, ComfyUI setup, EXR color metadata, DXF and cached-model caveats. Owner examples, agent judges, stars and source activity do not establish a recommendation. The Aseprite plugin is represented as optional packaging around its canonical server rather than counted twice. Web/manual shells, source failures and bounded captures are disclosed; retained research abstracts supply context without a claim of complete paper or manual review today. There is no fixed profile/discovery quota, platform preference, app execution or extra scheduler.  Verified collection: 1177/1177 repository queries attempted; 1174 successful, 2 partial, 1 failed and 255 bounded/truncated. All 27 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 53 watchlist fetches and 4 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

| Neural yarn modeling and machine knitting | The neural inverse-yarn primary abstract describes image-based procedural yarn inference. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.00154) |
| Neural watermarking for printed creative media | StegaStamp’s author page describes learned data embedding in physical photographs. [Source 1](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp) |
| Learned halftones and neural print screening | The retained inverse-halftoning primary abstract describes paired CNNs embedding recoverable color/detail into binary dot patterns. Practical print recovery, trained weights and software rights require separate evidence. At least one source capture is bounded by the2 MB response limit; it is not claimed as a complete document review. [Source 1](https://nikronic.com/projects/deep-halftoning_project/) [Source 2](https://ttwong12.github.io/papers/invhalftone/invhalftone.pdf) [Source 3](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2021/html/Xia_Deep_Halftoning_With_Reversible_Binary_Pattern_ICCV_2021_paper.html) |
| AI brick sculpture and agent-assisted construction design | BrickGPT and BrickWright’s primary pages describe generated buildable brick structures. [Source 1](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) |
| Neural optical holography and computational displays | Stanford/Princeton primary research and Odak/PADO documentation cover learned computer-generated holography. At least one source capture is bounded by the2 MB response limit; it is not claimed as a complete document review. [Source 1](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source 2](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source 3](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source 4](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/odak/learn_models/) [Source 5](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/installation/) [Source 6](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/cgh/) [Source 7](https://cinescope-wkr.github.io/pado-hologram/) |
| AI poetry, kinetic literature and language installations | RiTa’s current page explicitly calls its library AI-free. This is a contextual finding or explicit access/eligibility gap, rather than a newly admitted AI application. [Source 1](https://rednoise.org/rita/) [Source 2](https://jhave.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Cybefutu/j-space-theater) |
| Neural hair, fur and strand-groom authoring | HairGS, IPHG, StrandHead and HairGPT primary pages provide generation/reconstruction context. The retained Digital Salon guide separates learned retrieval, physics grooming, optional diffusion and separately governed data/meshes; no unchanged tool is presented as a new launch. [Source 1](https://github.com/yimin-pan/hair-gs) [Source 2](https://xiaokunsun.github.io/StrandHead.github.io/) [Source 3](https://haiminluo.github.io/hairgpt/) [Source 4](https://github.com/facebookresearch/iphg) [Source 5](https://digital-salon.github.io/) |
| Neural smoke, fluid capture and editable VFX fields | HyFluid’s author page and the PICT_smoke repository were checked; the separate PICT project-site URL returns 404. Current direct primary-source attempts include1 unavailable/access-limited response(s); affected claims remain unverified from those attempts. [Source 1](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/) [Source 2](https://19reborn.github.io/PICT_smoke/) [Source 3](https://github.com/19reborn/PICT_smoke) |
| AI pattern transcription and symbolic craft verification | StitchProof and Starlit connect model-assisted interpretation with deterministic pattern/count checking. A successful count check does not establish a physically correct garment or reliable model instruction reading. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B) [Source 2](https://stitchfoundry.github.io/StitchFoundry/) |
| Neural rhythm tracking and synchronized visual media | The BeatNet paper, Rust documentation and current package registry provide neural beat/downbeat context. The retained LyricsSync guide uses a separate Beat This! timing/manual-correction route; checkpoint and software grants remain distinct. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.03576) [Source 2](https://docs.rs/crate/beatnet-rs/latest/source/README.md) |
| Foundation-model dioramas and compositional scene assembly | Diorama’s primary page/released-code context describes foundation-model perception, CAD retrieval and layout optimization. Current pixel/SDF dioramas use a different agent-authored procedural route; neither inferred hidden geometry nor Gaussian surfels alone proves trained3D reconstruction. [Source 1](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/) |
| AI planner layouts, book arts and binding-aware publishing | Heritage Studio, eManuSkript and HornPenguin Booklet supply manuscript/layout context. Conventional imposition is not AI. The retained BoxCreator workflow and current PaperWorldPhoto prompt guide have distinct output boundaries: packaging geometry versus illustrated paper aesthetics, neither automatically physically proven. [Source 1](https://zenodo.org/records/18999747) [Source 2](https://www.rosette.dev/docs) [Source 3](https://jonidimo.github.io/agenda-heritage-generator/) [Source 4](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jonidimo/agenda-heritage-generator/main/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/baojiachen0214/BoxCreator) [Source 6](https://docs.hornpenguin.com/projects/booklet/en/latest/index.html) |
| AI shadow puppetry and hand-shadow performance | Live sources distinguish ShadowLite speech/LLM interaction, agent-authored shadow styles and the HaSPeR recognition dataset/teaching prototype. Retained full-source review found HaSPeR scope conflicting between rootMIT and a noncommercial README notice; no new clearance is inferred. Sketchry SHADOWPLAY free/account access is not an open-source grant, and the CUNY Wayang page is proposal context. [Source 1](https://github.com/QuentinCrane/ShadowLite) [Source 2](https://github.com/BubbleBrainBB/BB-Video) [Source 3](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025W/WCCA/papers/Raiyan_HaSPeR_An_Image_Repository_for_Hand_Shadow_Puppet_Recognition_ICCVW_2025_paper.pdf) [Source 4](https://sketchry.app/skills/shadowplay/) [Source 5](https://github.com/Starscream-11813/HaSPeR/blob/main/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Starscream-11813/HaSPeR/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 7](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/ny_pubs/1252/) [Source 8](https://openreview.net/forum?id=77b0dc11d1a9b57b3baf4b168cc2f00a42278f67) |
| AI-agent pixel art, sprites and palette-controlled animation | Live Pixelbolt primary documentation and current source reviews distinguish AI-agent-authored JSON/JavaScript/Python recipes from deterministic rasterization and conventional simulations. Guides cover exact-palette editing, Aseprite hosts and cloud-assisted sprite cleanup. The owner’s older benchmark did not establish its claimed advantage threshold. GitLab gridfab was identified as a human/AI text-art editor, but its full README/grant/API attempts returned 429, so it remains unadmitted. Gaussian appearance or AI-made screenshots alone do not establish a concrete AI tool. [Source 1](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 2](https://github.com/uilianks/pxl) [Source 3](https://gitlab.com/azrazalea/gridfab) [Source 4](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/compare/claude-pixel-art-with-and-without-pixelbolt/) |

## Search and review counts

108 creative fields; 1177/1177 repository queries attempted; 27/27 model-task queries attempted. 83112 distinct source candidates and 1174 model leads. 30 detailed profiles and 52 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 1 failed repository queries, 2 partial repository queries, 255 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Live topic listings and official file APIs supplement GitHub discovery. Five existing projects recovered full grants/documentation: openedit, agent-caption, PersoAI, JoyAI MLX and OpenDub. Explicit follow-up closes their code-license checks without quality promotions. Deeper file requests, gridfab and OpenSlides still return429/loading shells; Xotion’s current PolyForm Noncommercial grant is ineligible. This is bounded research, not a full host census. [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-generation?sort=stars_desc) [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-editor) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit) [Source](https://gitlab.com/azrazalea/gridfab) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **codeberg** · searched: Imaginer and Raspberry Pi diffusion repository context was revisited. Five requested source/license files returned608-byte Cookie monster bot checks despite HTTP200, while diffusion/audio searches returned429. Challenges and failures were archived without bypass; affected current checks remain unknown. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=25) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=audio&limit=25)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Current diffuser Cargo and full-license pages were revalidated. The manifest’s author-local absolute dependency remains a portable-setup gap in the retained assessment. Search visibility is limited; one accessible source does not establish complete host discovery or an independently reproduced creative workflow. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Live npm/PyPI/Rust metadata supports discovery without installing packages. Current score-reader metadata describes offline OMR/model/Java decoding; mcp-score-server documents an agent notation route. The beatnet-rs registry label alone is not a complete software grant. Package publication/download counts do not validate workflow quality or license all models. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/score-reader/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/mcp-score-server/json) [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/beatnet-rs) [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/litert-ocr/latest)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Primary Krita/REAPER/SAT/Max-related sources supplement current Aseprite, Unity, Animate and ComfyUI integrations. Software grants remain distinct from proprietary creative hosts, model weights and remote services. Current first-hand reports expose Windows plugin discovery and EXR chromaticity problems; owner tests and a closed issue do not prove complete reproduction. [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/) [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://github.com/willibrandon/pixel-plugin/issues/3) [Source](https://github.com/dughogan/DUGCO_Tools/issues/1) [Source](https://github.com/gekkyo/animate-mcp)
- **project-sites** · searched: Current primary-source attempts supplement all108 frozen fields, each with a non-GitHub source. Most retained bodies were revalidated; changed substantive sections and selected source documents were inspected. Illuminate recovered, while KinoDraw/MapStory/PICT/Feature3DGS and old manimvox/OpenSlides routes retain explicit failures. JavaScript shells and bounded/PDF captures are not claimed as complete manual/paper reviews. [Source](https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/illuminate/overview/) [Source](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source](https://handbook.uruworks.net) [Source](https://sketchry.app/skills/shadowplay/)
- **research-code** · searched: Research and toolkit material spans symbolic music, neural OCR, holography and event-assisted scene reconstruction. OCRacle’s current source records incomplete symbols/layout/non-Latin coverage; HFG checkpoints remain unreleased. Paper origin and owner measurements do not determine a tier or license the models/data; no discovered code was installed or run. [Source](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/models.md) [Source](https://github.com/HannahZhang1926/HFG-Neural-Holo) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11931) [Source](https://gitlab.com/algomus.fr/perso-ai/-/raw/master/README.md)
- **international** · searched: Current review includes Chinese-first typography, brick/paper art, storyboard production and Windows agent avatars; Japanese manga/Animate documentation, Korean gesture-studio UI and right-to-left motion-design material are also represented. Language, a browser frontend and popularity do not establish platform support, grant font/model rights or justify a quality label. [Source](https://github.com/Songzhi-lab/chinese-font-selector) [Source](https://github.com/liangdabiao/dsh-brick-mosaic) [Source](https://github.com/ketle-man/comfyui-comic-creator) [Source](https://github.com/yc9954/motioncad) [Source](https://github.com/Ismail-Dilshad/tempoloom)

## Pixelbolt

AI-agent pixel art, sprites and palette-controlled animation · Games & production pipelines · Motion capture & character animation

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making small editable sprites, tiles, interface icons and short pixel effects, with exact palette revisions and transparent exports for games or video composition.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner supplies source recipes, animated galleries and a controlled comparison on older version 0.11.0. That study did not meet its preregistered threshold for claiming an advantage over direct agent coding; it is not independent proof of current 0.17 output quality. Inspect every frame and loop at native resolution. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the official portable Studio archive for Windows x64, Linux x64/ARM64 or macOS Intel/Apple Silicon, or clone the source with Node 20+. The portable build bundles Node 24 LTS. No npm install or image-model download is needed for the renderer. Connect your chosen AI host separately; the optional skills CLI has a distinct Node 22.20+ requirement and telemetry behavior. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Current release/commit and bounded issue/PR evidence were inspected for workflow support. The query returned 10 records, including owner/bot items; it is not an exhaustive issue survey and establishes no general support/reliability clearance. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/pixelbolt/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** No successful independent use of the complete selected workflow was established in the bounded support/primary-document review. Owner automation, bots, adjacent-workflow reports and popularity are not substituted. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/pixelbolt/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete MIT software grant was reviewed; the README also covers original included artwork under MIT. There are no renderer model weights. The chosen AI host/model and any imported media retain separate terms. The renderer is local and needs no hosted inference service or key. A chosen AI authoring host can require its own account, model compute or paid access; optional paid effect packs are separate from the MIT toolkit. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Animation frames and imports are limited to 256x256; projects have at most 256 frames and 4,194,304 source pixels. A single still can reach 4096 pixels per side within the total; scenes and exports have additional limits. Advisory validation does not judge aesthetics. MCP calls have time/memory/output limits; large exports can require the CLI. URL sharing and embedded recipes carry source text. No universal RAM, VRAM or disk minimum is documented.

### How it uses AI

An AI agent authors and patches editable palette-based JSON through CLI or MCP tools; Pixelbolt deterministically rasterizes sprites, scenes and animations and returns pixel grids/contact sheets for the next agent revision. Its fluid and rigid-body effects are conventional simulations, not image-model inference. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

### Introduction

An AI agent authors and patches editable palette-based JSON through CLI or MCP tools; Pixelbolt deterministically rasterizes sprites, scenes and animations and returns pixel grids/contact sheets for the next agent revision. Its fluid and rigid-body effects are conventional simulations, not image-model inference. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

### What it is good for

Making small editable sprites, tiles, interface icons and short pixel effects, with exact palette revisions and transparent exports for games or video composition. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

### Demo & examples

The owner supplies source recipes, animated galleries and a controlled comparison on older version 0.11.0. That study did not meet its preregistered threshold for claiming an advantage over direct agent coding; it is not independent proof of current 0.17 output quality. Inspect every frame and loop at native resolution. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

### Install

Use the official portable Studio archive for Windows x64, Linux x64/ARM64 or macOS Intel/Apple Silicon, or clone the source with Node 20+. The portable build bundles Node 24 LTS. No npm install or image-model download is needed for the renderer. Connect your chosen AI host separately; the optional skills CLI has a distinct Node 22.20+ requirement and telemetry behavior. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official portable Studio archive for Windows x64, Linux x64/ARM64 or macOS Intel/Apple Silicon, or clone the source with Node 20+. The portable build bundles Node 24 LTS. No npm install or image-model download is needed for the renderer. Connect your chosen AI host separately; the optional skills CLI has a distinct Node 22.20+ requirement and telemetry behavior.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt.git
```


```sh
cd Pixelbolt
```


```sh
node bin/pixelbolt.js preview --open
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

1. Open the local studio, choose a small supplied recipe and save your own JSON copy.
2. Have your agent alter a palette, one region or a short motion; validate the recipe and inspect its native-size frames, silhouette and loop.
3. Render into a new output folder, keeping the JSON recipe and atlas with the PNG/APNG assets. GIF export can alter alpha and timing.
4. For a video editor, export a constant-rate numbered PNG sequence and set that rate on import. Pixelbolt supplies FFmpeg commands but does not itself encode an MP4.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

- **Hardware:** A CPU renderer is documented without a GPU/image-model requirement. Minimum RAM and total storage are not documented. The portable runtime download size is not a system-memory minimum.
- **Software:** Node 20+ for source/CLI/MCP, or a portable Studio build with its runtime. A separate compatible AI agent is the creative author. FFmpeg is optional for turning exported frames into video.
- **Platforms:** Windows, macOS and Linux are explicitly documented, with five portable OS/architecture targets. CI content checks do not prove a human double-click, download-warning or every desktop/browser route works.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The complete MIT software grant was reviewed; the README also covers original included artwork under MIT. There are no renderer model weights. The chosen AI host/model and any imported media retain separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The complete MIT software grant was reviewed; the README also covers original included artwork under MIT. There are no renderer model weights. The chosen AI host/model and any imported media retain separate terms.
- **Cost:** The renderer is local and needs no hosted inference service or key. A chosen AI authoring host can require its own account, model compute or paid access; optional paid effect packs are separate from the MIT toolkit.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

### Limitations

Animation frames and imports are limited to 256x256; projects have at most 256 frames and 4,194,304 source pixels. A single still can reach 4096 pixels per side within the total; scenes and exports have additional limits. Advisory validation does not judge aesthetics. MCP calls have time/memory/output limits; large exports can require the CLI. URL sharing and embedded recipes carry source text. No universal RAM, VRAM or disk minimum is documented. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://skulitom.github.io/Pixelbolt/) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Pixelbolt/blob/main/package.json)

Lint detects specified pixel patterns, not taste. Arbitrary art source is code and belongs in a trusted local project. ### How it uses AI

A coding agent writes a compact JavaScript/TypeScript pixel-art scene, renders it through a deterministic DSL, inspects exact grids and lint overlays, and patches the source. The fork adds hue-shift ramps, selective outlines and annotated human feedback; its rendering and lint are not learned image generation. [Source](https://github.com/uilianks/pxl/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/uilianks/pxl/blob/main/reference.md)

### How it uses AI

### Install

[Source 1](https://github.com/ljhljh0703-cmd/codex-asset-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ljhljh0703-cmd/codex-asset-forge/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ljhljh0703-cmd/codex-asset-forge/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the source and use an isolated Python 3.10+ environment, then install the editable package with Pillow 10+. Begin with a supplied or owned draft and new output paths.

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
```


```sh
source .venv/bin/activate
```


```sh
python -m pip install -e .
```


```sh
codex-asset-forge key examples/agentforge-starter/raw/tileset_raw.png /tmp/tileset_keyed.png
```


```sh
codex-asset-forge slice-grid /tmp/tileset_keyed.png /tmp/tiles --columns 2 --rows 2 --prefix tile
```


```sh
codex-asset-forge manifest-grid /tmp/tileset_keyed.png /tmp/tileset.json --columns 2 --rows 2 --layer base
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ljhljh0703-cmd/codex-asset-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ljhljh0703-cmd/codex-asset-forge/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ljhljh0703-cmd/codex-asset-forge/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Retain the unmodified generated draft and confirm that its background is appropriate for chroma keying.
2. Key a copy and inspect alpha edges and any subject colors close to the background.
3. Slice using the actual grid and write a manifest with the intended layer; verify rectangles and anchors.
4. Import into the destination runtime and inspect actual motion, placement and collision behavior; keep human acceptance separate from file/renderer proof.

### How it uses AI

An AI agent uses an explicit Chinese scenario/mood matrix and CJK/Latin pairing rules to propose a three-level type hierarchy. This is a text knowledge skill, not a font generator or a font license validator. 4. Render a representative layout and inspect line breaking, punctuation, hierarchy and missing glyphs.

Units, thread sequencing, density, machine limits and material behavior are unvalidated. Do not use the missing-target batch launcher or infer printing/sewing readiness from an export.

### How it uses AI

ai_emb.py learns a per-image KMeans color palette, finds contours and emits rotated hatch geometry as SVG. This is classical image clustering plus conventional geometry, not neural stitch generation. Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. 3. Inspect SVG color regions, tiny contours and hatch density at the intended dimensions.
4. Treat any eventual stitch conversion, stabilization and physical sew-out as a separate unverified process.

Cancellation can leave attached engine compute running even when outputs are not published.

### How it uses AI

A native macOS frontend runs local ACE-Step 1.5 song generation from prompts/lyrics, with takes, seed variations, loop/repaint edits and a headless CLI sharing the GUI core. ### How it uses AI

An AI coding agent operates a structured local editing CLI; optional faster-whisper transcribes speech, while plans, cuts, captions and FFmpeg renders are deterministic editing operations. ### Install

Use Python 3.10+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and a new virtual environment. Install moviestar[transcribe] for local speech recognition; the base package deliberately omits Whisper/PyAV/CTranslate2/ONNX dependencies. [Source 1](https://github.com/jdilla1277/moviestar/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jdilla1277/moviestar/blob/main/docs/getting-started.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/jdilla1277/moviestar/blob/main/docs/installation.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.10+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and a new virtual environment. Install moviestar[transcribe] for local speech recognition; the base package deliberately omits Whisper/PyAV/CTranslate2/ONNX dependencies.

```sh
python -m pip install --upgrade "moviestar[transcribe]"
```


```sh
moviestar doctor
```


```sh
moviestar probe /path/to/video.mp4
```


```sh
moviestar load /path/to/video.mp4 --as source
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/jdilla1277/moviestar/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jdilla1277/moviestar/blob/main/docs/getting-started.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/jdilla1277/moviestar/blob/main/docs/installation.md)

1. Use a dedicated working directory and probe a short owned source.
2. Find the desired spoken phrase and use the returned time range to trim with word snapping.
3. Review export --dry-run before rendering into a fresh destination.
4. Inspect the exported video and audio; a screenshot/probe alone does not verify motion or sound.

- **Software:** Python 3.10+, FFmpeg and ffprobe; transcription extra/model download only when requested. Account/cloud metadata uses a different lighter profile.
- **Platforms:** Local Python/FFmpeg workflow; macOS VideoToolbox fallback is explicitly documented. A complete tested OS/codec matrix is not established.

Wizards card text/art and scans are not licensed by the code grant. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/models.md) [Source](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/guide.md) [Source](https://cmeister2.github.io/ocracle/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current project explicitly remains experimental. Six Latin-script languages have stronger medium-baseline evidence; Latin la and Japanese/Chinese remain pending/best effort. Mana symbols, vertical/curved text, whole-card layout and severe blur are incomplete; RAM/VRAM/storage minima are undocumented.

### How it uses AI

Local Rust CTC neural OCR reads printed Magic card pixels, including historic typography and italics. Language profiles choose scoped recognizers; it does not substitute a card database lookup for observed text. ### Install

Clone the source, use Rust 1.94+ and Python 3 for the initial hash-pinned model downloader, then run the recognize CLI. Recognition runs locally after required model downloads. [Source 1](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/models.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/guide.md) [Source 4](https://cmeister2.github.io/ocracle/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the source, use Rust 1.94+ and Python 3 for the initial hash-pinned model downloader, then run the recognize CLI. Recognition runs locally after required model downloads.

```sh
python3 training/download_models.py
```


```sh
cargo run --release -- recognize card.jpg
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/models.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cmeister2/ocracle/blob/main/docs/guide.md) [Source 4](https://cmeister2.github.io/ocracle/)

1. Begin with an owned, upright crop and the correct printed-language profile.
2. Use --line for an already cropped line; retain transforms if integrating a camera pipeline.
3. Compare JSON transcription, coordinates and actual model identities against the printed reference.
4. Keep accents, punctuation and severe-blur failures visible; confidence is an uncalibrated model score.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/dewayneku/pixel-forge.git
```


```sh
cd pixel-forge
```

```sh
npm install
```

```sh
npm start
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/dewayneku/pixel-forge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open localhost:7777 and create a small sprite or import an owned sheet.
2. Connect a compatible agent and request one layer/palette change before animation.
3. Preview each frame; shared undo also reverses agent edits and omitted frame/layer arguments use the active selection.
4. Save a .forge.json project and inspect PNG/GIF or sheet/JSON exports in the intended engine.

### How it uses AI

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Procedural pixel rendering is distinct from learned image generation. Native image I/O, MCP/client compatibility, maximum practical scene sizes, hardware minima and independent results remain unverified. The owner’s zero-latency wording is not a physical performance guarantee.

### How it uses AI

An LLM turns a scene description into structured MCP drawing/character/motion calls. A deterministic TypeScript engine composes procedural sprites, palettes and frame sequences; no image model runs inside that engine. [Source](https://github.com/rxolve/pixscii/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rxolve/pixscii/blob/main/package.json)

## Tero Subtitler — local transcription and subtitle revision

Editing, captions & post-production · Accessible media & assistive creation

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Drafting accessible subtitles from local speech, then manually correcting words, language changes, timing and line breaks in a subtitle editor.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** An independent report posted in 2025 describes regular faster-whisper/batch subtitling on multilingual episodes, with missed quiet/noisy speech even with medium/large-v3. Rechecking that source today establishes historical use, not an independent reproduction of the current UW backend or a measured accuracy pass. The source UI exposes smart segmentation, mixed-language detection and speaker labels; these controls are not a guarantee of correct dialogue or identity. [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_FasterWhisper/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_WhisperCPP/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.pas) [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.lfm) [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/releases) [Source](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/issues/447)

### Install

Obtain the official Tero package for the intended platform and inspect its release designation: the captured latest item is nightly 1.0.2.4, not a verified stable release. The stable-release API returned 404. The owner supplies standalone whisper-faster backend archives that Tero downloads separately, plus model downloads. For the explicit backend route, obtain the matching official backend archive, prepare a local PCM WAV and use the documented command below; open the resulting SRT with the matching media in Tero for revision. FFmpeg/mpv and backend/model paths must be available for the integrated route. No installer or backend was run during this review. [Source 1](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_FasterWhisper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_WhisperCPP/blob/main/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.pas) [Source 5](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.lfm) [Source 6](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/releases) [Source 7](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/issues/447)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the official Tero package for the intended platform and inspect its release designation: the captured latest item is nightly 1.0.2.4, not a verified stable release. The stable-release API returned 404. The owner supplies standalone whisper-faster backend archives that Tero downloads separately, plus model downloads. For the explicit backend route, obtain the matching official backend archive, prepare a local PCM WAV and use the documented command below; open the resulting SRT with the matching media in Tero for revision. FFmpeg/mpv and backend/model paths must be available for the integrated route. No installer or backend was run during this review.

```sh
whisper-faster audio.wav --model medium --model_dir ./models --output_dir ./out --output_format srt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_FasterWhisper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_WhisperCPP/blob/main/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.pas) [Source 5](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.lfm) [Source 6](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/releases) [Source 7](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/issues/447)

1. Use a short local WAV with representative speech and language changes. The standalone binary expects PCM WAV and resamples to 16 kHz mono; arbitrary container input requires the source/PyAV route, rather than being assumed for the prebuilt binary.
2. Choose the backend/model explicitly. Missing faster-whisper models download from Hugging Face. Open the SRT in Tero with the corresponding local media and revise text, timing, segmentation and export format.
3. For integrated transcription, the inspected Audio to text form has Engine, Model, Language and Track controls, a model-download button, Generate, and load-after/add-while-transcribing choices. Its implementation rejects HTTP media for this operation and requires FFmpeg and the selected backend; use a local file.
4. For code-switched speech, the documented faster-whisper mixed-language option detects each 30-second window; do not force one language for the whole clip. Translate to English changes the task and is distinct from transcription in the source language.
5. Check quiet lines, noise, repetitions and speaker attribution against the original audio. Preserve a revised subtitle copy and verify timing/line breaks in the target player; an automatic QC profile does not certify accessibility or translation quality.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_FasterWhisper/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/URUWorks/UW_WhisperCPP/blob/main/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.pas) [Source 5](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/blob/main/TeroSubtitler/src/formAudioToText.lfm) [Source 6](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/releases) [Source 7](https://github.com/URUWorks/TeroSubtitler/issues/447)

- **Hardware:** System RAM, VRAM and total application/model storage minima are not documented for this complete workflow. The optional Windows CUDA library download is about 1.3 GB and the optional OpenVINO add-on about 200 MB; these are download sizes, not RAM minima.
- **Software:** The prebuilt backend avoids a source Python installation. Optional NVIDIA faster-whisper acceleration needs CUDA 12/cuDNN 9; auto can fall back to CPU and logs the reason. Its macOS route runs on CPU. The distinct whisper.cpp wrapper supports Metal on Apple Silicon and Vulkan on Windows/Linux x64; these are not faster-whisper CUDA on Mac. Tero source compilation uses Lazarus plus the separately listed internal/external packages.
- **Platforms:** The app owner declares Windows/macOS/Linux; app minimum OS versions and complete device testing are not established here. Faster-whisper backend packages separately declare Windows x64, macOS 11+ Intel/Apple Silicon and Linux x64 glibc 2.35+. Its legacy Intel macOS 10.11–10.15 build omits VAD and speaker identification; backend support does not establish that the entire current GUI runs on those older systems.

The repository was created on 2026-11-17. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making temporal video sculptures, depth trails and transparent VFX plates from owned clips; this is an artistic reconstruction route rather than calibrated surveying.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner links a tutorial and examples for depth points, object following and ordinary frame stacks. Owner synthetic tests report camera-path and mask errors on particular rendered scenes; those are not independent results on arbitrary footage. The current depth.py stretches relative predictions between per-frame quantiles and smooths them, so its scene units are not measured metres. [Source](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/depth.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the owner-documented Windows 10/11 route with Python 3.11+, Chrome or Edge/WebGL2, and ffmpeg/ffprobe on PATH. The basic cube needs no Python third-party packages. For neural depth/Track, first install a CUDA-enabled PyTorch matching the NVIDIA device using the linked upstream selector, then the requirements file; launch from that environment. The separate caption environment and Qwen file are optional and are not needed for a first depth-trail plate. No setup command was executed here. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. ### How it uses AI

Local Depth Anything depth estimation and CLIPSeg/SAM object selection turn video frames into sculptural stacks and point trails. The procedural cube is separate from optional neural depth, tracking and Qwen3-VL captions. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the owner-documented Windows 10/11 route with Python 3.11+, Chrome or Edge/WebGL2, and ffmpeg/ffprobe on PATH. The basic cube needs no Python third-party packages. For neural depth/Track, first install a CUDA-enabled PyTorch matching the NVIDIA device using the linked upstream selector, then the requirements file; launch from that environment.
```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python launch.pyw
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/depth.py)

1. Open a short local video. Start with the basic cube to check decoding and choose its In/Out range; brightness-derived relief is a conventional fallback, not AI depth.
2. Choose Build AI depth, wait for its separate model download/build and inspect depth edges. The current code uses LiheYoung/depth-anything-large-hf, rather than assuming this is Depth Anything V2.
3. Try Depth points or Depth sheets. For subject isolation, pause on a clear object, Find it with text or click a point, then Follow it; the tracker may release the object when it leaves or becomes uncertain.
4. Use Camera path only as an artistic estimate: the default 65-degree field of view is assumed, and depth is relative. Orbit/Walk/Turn are synthetic paths and are explicitly different from the measured-from-video option.
5. Choose frame-by-frame export for a plate. Opaque export is H.264 MP4; the documented transparent route is ProRes 4444 MOV or VP9-alpha WebM. Live Record stays opaque. Check alpha, timing, contours and sound in the actual receiving editor.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/mariobilly/time-cube/blob/main/depth.py)

- **Hardware:** Minimum whole-workflow RAM, depth VRAM and total storage are not documented. Depth weights download about 1.3 GB, CLIPSeg/SAM about 1 GB together. Optional Qwen captions separately need about 6 GB free graphics memory and a 4.9 GB file; that is not a universal minimum for depth or the basic cube.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+, ffmpeg/ffprobe and Chrome/Edge with WebGL2. Neural requirements are unpinned torch, NumPy/Pillow/OpenCV/SciPy/accelerate/safetensors plus transformers>=4.56,<5; Qwen captions separately require transformers 4.57. Independent dependency compatibility is unverified.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 is declared and Windows 11 owner-tested. A browser window does not establish Mac/Linux support. NVIDIA/CUDA is explicitly required by the selected depth implementation; CPU-only procedural rendering is a different route.

### How it uses AI

A local JSON/Rust/MCP editing engine lets an AI agent arrange recorded footage, revise a saved timeline, add captions/graphics, preview and export. It edits existing media rather than generating new video from a prompt. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

### Introduction

A local JSON/Rust/MCP editing engine lets an AI agent arrange recorded footage, revise a saved timeline, add captions/graphics, preview and export. It edits existing media rather than generating new video from a prompt. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

### What it is good for

Building inspectable interview, screen-tutorial and presentation edits with retained media and revision history. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

### Demo & examples

Owner evidence describes frame-exact cuts, retry/undo checks and repeat exports on a particular FFmpeg build. The linked competitor comparison includes source/document reviews for some alternatives, not equal hands-on trials for every tool. Independent full creative-workflow use remains unverified. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

### Install

For the scoped Windows/Linux source route, obtain the official source, use the owner-documented Rust edition2024-compatible toolchain plus C compiler/linker for SQLite, and provide FFmpeg/ffprobe on PATH or their explicit CUTBOLT variables. Install the locked project and inspect doctor output. Its setup-ready flag is not a render, GPU, speech or model test. The installation document still says binaries are in preparation while README describes early releases; this guide deliberately scopes source setup rather than treating the two as equivalent. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For the scoped Windows/Linux source route, obtain the official source, use the owner-documented Rust edition2024-compatible toolchain plus C compiler/linker for SQLite, and provide FFmpeg/ffprobe on PATH or their explicit CUTBOLT variables. Install the locked project and inspect doctor output. Its setup-ready flag is not a render, GPU, speech or model test. The installation document still says binaries are in preparation while README describes early releases; this guide deliberately scopes source setup rather than treating the two as equivalent.

```sh
cargo install --path . --locked
```


```sh
cutbolt --version
```


```sh
cutbolt doctor
```


```sh
cutbolt --workspace /absolute/path/to/workspace mcp
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

1. Create a separate media/output workspace and connect the stdio server from the same environment that finds FFmpeg. Keep the selected source commit and media-tool build with the project.
2. Ask the agent to inspect an owned clip, trim its first ten seconds, save the edit and show a preview. This is the owner’s first-use task, not a completed test by AI Media Scout.
3. Use the saved-session commands for shared revisions, retry receipts and undo; a pure timeline.apply result can fork when two callers edit the same supplied snapshot and is not a shared session lock.
4. Inspect the preview and exported sound/timing. Optional speech recognition needs its own environment/models; a connected MCP endpoint or doctor-ready result does not establish transcription readiness.
5. Review a small final export before a long job. The documented MCP export/background path needs Windows or Linux; an available macOS ARM64 executable alone does not clear that whole workflow.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Rust edition2024-compatible source toolchain, C compiler/linker and external FFmpeg/ffprobe. Python, speech checkpoints, CUDA/WSL and interchange tools are optional workflow/development dependencies, not universal core requirements.
- **Platforms:** Windows x64 and Linux x64 are current verification platforms. macOS ARM64 archives exist, but background/MCP export and Windows-specific engine features have narrower support. Client/plugin coverage is also version- and platform-scoped.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Full MIT engine grant reviewed. FFmpeg/codecs, fonts, optional Whisper/CTC weights, proprietary GPU drivers and the selected agent host retain separate terms. The dependency ledger’s RTX4090/WSL speech setup is one tested owner profile, not a required or independently reproduced minimum.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Full MIT engine grant reviewed. FFmpeg/codecs, fonts, optional Whisper/CTC weights, proprietary GPU drivers and the selected agent host retain separate terms. The dependency ledger’s RTX4090/WSL speech setup is one tested owner profile, not a required or independently reproduced minimum.
- **Cost:** No hosted-service account is required by the core local engine. Hardware and optional model/agent/provider costs are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/INSTALLATION.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Cutbolt/blob/main/docs/DEPENDENCIES.md)

### Limitations

The core default is 8-bit SDR; optional working-space/effect/source/interchange paths have separate contracts. Setup-ready, source preservation, deterministic receipts and owner acceptance tests do not establish output quality, current cross-client usability or independent support. Full APIs and the entire dependency ledger were not audited line by line; the guide covers the cited core setup/session route. ## Nestbolt — agent-assisted fixed-sheet nesting

3D printing & generative CAD · AI-assisted textile and computational craft

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Arranging fixed-sheet laser/CNC/textile parts while preserving quantities, spacing, rotations and source geometry for CAM review.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-17. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The owner’s frozen v1 benchmark reports 8.5% median material improvement over selected alternatives at 60 seconds, yet misses its own90%-wins/ties bar; Sparrow still wins some cases. An independent geometry validator is a separate algorithm, not an independent creative user or proof of physical cutting. The newer preview has no new like-for-like competitor claim. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** For the scoped basic SVG/DXF MCP route, obtain the official stable v0.1.0 native archive for Windows x64, Linux x64 or macOS Apple Silicon, verify checksums and place the executable on the intended path. Register the absolute nestbolt-mcp path and set its workspace to the cutting-files folder. A browser UI is optional; it runs a newer preview and is not the same version. Building current source instead selects Rust 1.98.1 with a Cargo minimum1.98 and may fetch large benchmark/upstream data. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Current release/commit and bounded issue/PR evidence were inspected for workflow support. The query returned 10 records, including owner/bot items; it is not an exhaustive issue survey and establishes no general support/reliability clearance. A third-party library integration reports current DXF import defects around negative-Z extrusion, paper-space inserts and MINSERT. Separate reports cover zero-length exported LINEs and a multi-GB Git dependency checkout. These are concrete import/library limits, not proof of every MCP nesting path. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/nestbolt/issues) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/nestbolt/issues/15)
- **Independent use · unknown:** No successful independent use of the complete selected workflow was established in the bounded support/primary-document review. Owner automation, bots, adjacent-workflow reports and popularity are not substituted. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/nestbolt/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete MIT project grant reviewed. jagua-rs remains MPL2; adapted Sparrow code is MIT. LightBurn/CAM hosts, stock data, fonts/artwork and the chosen AI client retain separate terms. The geometric MCP server itself documents no network/account/key requirement. Local optimization has no documented per-call service fee; CAM software, material, machine operation and any external AI host are separate. [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Stable v0.1.0, the alpha preview, current hosted main and the frozen benchmark are different builds. The optional common-line route can increase material use and needs CAM preparation. The existing independent library integration reports negative-Z/paperspace/MINSERT and zero-length DXF issues, not validation of the full selected MCP workflow. Full physical cutting, independent end-to-end setup and current creative quality remain unverified. A third-party library integration reports current DXF import defects around negative-Z extrusion, paper-space inserts and MINSERT. Separate reports cover zero-length exported LINEs and a multi-GB Git dependency checkout. These are concrete import/library limits, not proof of every MCP nesting path.

### How it uses AI

A local MCP interface lets an AI agent import part outlines, choose stock/constraints, call a deterministic nesting optimizer, validate and export cutting layouts. The optimizer is geometric rather than a neural model. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

1. Start with the owner’s simple SVG parts in a separate workspace. Call import_parts and inspect units, part ids, holes, repairs and stock outlines before nesting.
2. Supply quantities, sheet sizes/margins and spacing in millimetres, plus permitted rotations/grain direction and mirroring. estimate_sheets is only an area lower bound, never a proven feasible layout.
3. Call nest with a bounded budget and seed, then validate_layout and export_layout. The five-tool interface is the same core as the CLI; a reported real-time limit can end deterministic-repeatability guarantees.
4. Inspect each SVG/DXF in the actual CAM program and verify scale, contours and tool setup. Material geometry validity is not a machine-ready cut plan.
5. Treat editable LightBurn import, lead allowances, remnant handling and common-line cutting as separately versioned preview/development workflows. Their limitations and current third-party DXF import reports must not be transferred into a blanket claim that the stable basic route is tested on every shop file.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Prebuilt native tools need an MCP client/agent, with sufficient call timeout for up to 300 seconds. Optional source uses Rust 1.98.1 (Cargo declares 1.98 minimum); local WebAssembly UI development adds Node 24 and wasm-bindgen-cli0.2.129. No universal RAM/VRAM/storage minimum is documented.
- **Platforms:** Native archives are published for Windows x64, Linux x64 and macOS Apple Silicon. Owner client checks have narrower platform/version scope; accepting one DeepSeek configuration was not an actual server start without that host’s key.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Complete MIT project grant reviewed. jagua-rs remains MPL2; adapted Sparrow code is MIT. LightBurn/CAM hosts, stock data, fonts/artwork and the chosen AI client retain separate terms. The geometric MCP server itself documents no network/account/key requirement.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Complete MIT project grant reviewed. jagua-rs remains MPL2; adapted Sparrow code is MIT. LightBurn/CAM hosts, stock data, fonts/artwork and the chosen AI client retain separate terms. The geometric MCP server itself documents no network/account/key requirement.
- **Cost:** Local optimization has no documented per-call service fee; CAM software, material, machine operation and any external AI host are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

### Limitations

Stable v0.1.0, the alpha preview, current hosted main and the frozen benchmark are different builds. The optional common-line route can increase material use and needs CAM preparation. The existing independent library integration reports negative-Z/paperspace/MINSERT and zero-length DXF issues, not validation of the full selected MCP workflow. Full physical cutting, independent end-to-end setup and current creative quality remain unverified. A third-party library integration reports current DXF import defects around negative-Z extrusion, paper-space inserts and MINSERT. Separate reports cover zero-length exported LINEs and a multi-GB Git dependency checkout. These are concrete import/library limits, not proof of every MCP nesting path. [Source 1](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/docs/AGENT_INTERFACE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source 4](https://github.com/skulitom/nestbolt/issues/15)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/skulitom/Nestbolt/blob/main/README.md)

### Aseprite MCP Tools (diivi) · MIT

Exploring AI-authored pixel-art scenes and animation with direct Aseprite feedback.
A Python MCP server lets an AI assistant draw, inspect, shade and animate editable Aseprite files through a large tool catalog and Lua operations.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete MIT code grant reviewed. Python 3.13+, uv and a separately licensed Aseprite are documented, with a 60-second default process timeout. Docker/Steam installation is a separate account-dependent route. Owner examples,123-tool coverage and star counts do not establish independent quality or every host/platform combination.

### pixelforge-mcp (freema) · MIT

Drafting sprite/tileset candidates and short generated frame chains.
A Node MCP server calls Gemini image generation, then conventionally crops, keys, pixelates and splits outputs into sprite assets.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete MIT server grant reviewed. Node 20+ and a separately billed/permitted Gemini key/model are required. Per-frame reference chains can incur multiple requests and do not guarantee character identity. Preview model aliases, chroma-key subject loss, matting and API availability require review; the server code license does not clear model/output rights.

### ssyubix pixel-art MCP · Apache-2.0

Building inspectable small game assets and correctly sized tile grids.
An AI agent uses a Python stdio MCP server to draw pixel canvases, create palettes and assemble tileset/map grids, optionally handing PNG/JSON metadata to Unity tools.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete Apache 2 grant reviewed. The drawing operations are conventional; external AI supplies decisions. Unity/companion MCP and artwork have separate terms. The README records a prior tile-versus-map sizing mistake and requires complete grid filling; exact Python/platform requirements, import behavior and independent results remain pending.

### Pixel Art MCP (uncagedspirit) · MIT

Drafting scene art and small asset studies from a chosen neural backend.
A Python MCP server sends prompts to an image backend, then uses Pillow downscaling/quantization to turn results into palette-limited PNG candidates; it can also pixelate supplied images.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete MIT code grant reviewed. Python 3.10+ and outbound HTTPS are documented; current availability, account requirements, costs and rights of the advertised community backend remain unverified. Palette/animation/transparent-output roadmap items are not shipped guarantees, and no screenshot or star count supplies a quality verdict.

### ThreadLore · MIT

Reconstructing candidate plain/twill weave drafts with human correction and explicit user-supplied provenance.
An implemented Vertex/Gemini client proposes weave structures from textile macro photos; a user-confirmed repeat tile feeds a deterministic four-shaft/four-treadle WIF compiler.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete MIT grant reviewed. Python 3.11+/Node 20+, FastAPI/OpenCV and Google Cloud/Vertex access are documented. The client labels its0.60 confidence threshold uncalibrated and falls back to manual selection on failure. Matrix round-trip equality does not prove physical weaving, structural suitability or accurate identification; independent loom use is unknown. Owner print/touch/preview issue-and-fix activity is not independent testing. The README schedules a November20launch, so this is not a claim it has already launched. Current model availability, cloud costs and photo/privacy handling need further review.

### Alter Effects · Apache-2.0

Planning and reviewing recipe-based motion graphics with editable captions, frame notes and explicit approval.
Vision tags and language-model storyboards fill versioned motion-design recipes; deterministic Chromium/FFmpeg rendering and on-device Whisper/MediaPipe tools support footage-led videos.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete Apache 2 grant reviewed. Node 20+/Docker, Postgres, S3-compatible storage, render workers and optional provider accounts are distinct dependencies. The recipe example can run without AI, while storyboard/tagging uses configured models. Source-declared gates and claims checking do not verify factual accuracy or creative quality. GSAP uses its own Standard license; codecs, model weights and supplied media are separate. The bounded support result contains eight Dependabot updates, not independent first-hand creative use. Full setup, costs and output review remain pending.

### promo-capture — AI-agent recording component · MIT

Supplying synchronized, reproducible capture steps to an AI-directed app-promo workflow.
Its published AI-agent contract and JSON take specification let a coding agent orchestrate repeatable Android/iOS-simulator/window footage and read capture events for promo editing. Recording is deterministic, with no embedded neural model.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Full MIT grant and the explicit agent recording contract were reviewed as source data, not executed instructions. Python 3.9+, adb for Android, Xcode for iOS Simulator and macOS 15+/ScreenCaptureKit for window audio are scoped requirements; Windows-app capture here means CrossOver/Wine on Mac, not a native Windows recorder. Physical devices, permissions, capture timing and independent end-to-end use remain unverified. It is a capture component rather than an AI image generator.

### event-sync — AI-agent timing component · MIT

Measuring per-take screen/audio alignment in an AI-orchestrated promo-editing pipeline.
The explicit agent-facing JSON/exit-code contract lets an editing agent align separate media by visual events, sound onset and signal matching, then inspect final-video timing. Its core uses deterministic DSP rather than a neural synchronization model.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete MIT grant and the concrete agent media recipe were reviewed. Python 3.9+, NumPy and FFmpeg are documented; model/provider choice belongs to the external agent. ROI, onset threshold, variable frame rate and per-clip concatenation affect measurements; one successful tolerance check is not a perceptual or universal sync guarantee. Synthetic owner tests and an empty bounded issue result do not establish independent real-media accuracy. Full profile and wider device/backend review remain pending.

Developing scene continuity and reviewable generation packs before spending on video takes.

### Paper Cutout · MIT

Building stylized short paper-person explainers with editable scene JSON and owned portraits.
A Claude Code skill directs Python/HyperFrames paper-character animation, optional rembg segmentation and local Kokoro speech around a script and supplied face/voice.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Complete MIT code grant and source setup/voice route reviewed. Python 3.8+, Pillow, Node 20+ and FFmpeg are documented; optional rembg and kokoro-onnx/soundfile plus HyperFrames/browser/model downloads are separate. Windows 11 is owner-tested; Mac steps are explicitly untested. Default word timing is estimated from word length, not forced alignment, and two differently framed face photos can jump. The13-row scorecard checks the plan, not rendered pixels. A local renderer with no generation API key does not remove the Claude host account/usage requirement. Model/font/media terms and independent creative quality remain pending.

### ShaperBuild · GPL-3.0-or-later

Developing editable parts and scenic/object prototypes with AI-assisted parametric operations.
The CAD app exposes editable sketch/B-rep tools to its model-backed assistant and a local MCP bridge, translating language requests into ordinary undoable history steps.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The complete GPL3 operative terms and explicit version 3-or-later declaration were reviewed; only a nonoperative explanatory URL differs from the retained standard. Node 20+ source and Windows/macOS Intel/Apple Silicon/Linux packages are documented, with unsigned/ad-hoc package caveats. OpenCascade/LGPL exception, Manifold, fonts, hosted/local models and accounts retain separate conditions. Owner PR13 changes the Windows Claude bridge but explicitly lacks a real-Windows reproduction; Linux handshake/owner tests do not verify that route. STL/3MF export or a geometry check does not prove physical print readiness. Independent setup, fabrication and creative quality remain unverified.

Coordinating role-scoped agent edits on a shared talking-head or explainer timeline.

### Postbay — agent-assisted creative review · MIT

Coordinating frame-specific revision requests and version-bound approval for AI-produced media.
Its implemented MCP and webhook runner carry frame-anchored review requests into an AI agent’s source project, then collect a rendered revision and replies as a new version for review; drawn-by-code is the documented integration.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The complete MIT software grant was reviewed despite GitHub NOASSERTION; appended exclusions govern fonts, ElevenLabs sound assets and viewing-only renders separately. Node 22/PostgreSQL16/FFmpeg and selected agent/model access are documented. The producer runner cannot approve; an interactive MCP assistant acts with its signed-in user role and can approve only under the separately enabled brand policy. Every social connector is tested only against a local stand-in, with no real-account runs; nine network names do not establish publishing readiness. Model cost, OAuth roles, deployment, media rights and independent creative/workflow quality remain unverified.
